Specs the `CIGateVerdict` (one green tick clears the streak; a RED streak ≥ `Threshold` reds; any one persistently-RED visual reds the conservative roll-up) and the build wiring (a `*_test.go` folded by `go test ./...` — the `architest`/`pythongate`/`windowgate` shape — or a `scorecard-ratchet` `--check` like `covmatrix` `growth_debt`, `Makefile:229-233`, mirrored into `scripts/ci.ps1`). Consumes only C9 verdicts (zero new trust); emits the C13 DOS tokens / `CTX_SAFETY_VISUAL_DRIFT`. Stale Makefile line-refs adversarially corrected. Closes doctrine D + C2 gap G-A (#1219) at the build boundary. Research/design only.
- [Context-safety debug-window surface (#1229, C11 of epic #1217) (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/context-safety-debug-window-surface-1217.md) — the C11 surface spec for epic [#1217](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/1217): the HOME the C6 schemas asked for — where the six visual primitives live as panels, the data each binds to, and the read route each scrapes — landing a concrete instance into the generic #196 (Performance Dashboards) placeholder by hanging the panels off the context debugger `cmd/fak/debug.go:20-25` (the surface that attaches to a finished session and answers `context-query` / `context-diff` / `--prefer-view summary`). Specs the panel wireframe + per-panel data binding (cross-ref the C6 schemas, #1223) + the read route (`/metrics` `handleMetrics` `internal/gateway/metrics.go:1041`, `/debug/vars` `handleDebugVars` `debug.go:286-291`, registered in `http.go` `routeTable():51`), and the `(*Image).HTMLReport` (`cdb/report_html.go:91`) + `scoreboard/render.go:41` `FromPayload` render precedents. Defers RENDERING to C6 and re-derivation to C9. Citations adversarially verified (`routeTable()` line corrected). Owns placement + binding. Research/design only.
- [Context-safety liveness-contradiction light (#1230, C12 of epic #1217) (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/context-safety-liveness-light-1217.md) — the C12 spec for primitive (6) of epic [#1217](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/1217): the liveness-contradiction light that catches the #1143 "healthy but serving nothing" failure (GDN evict panic: `/healthz` answers 200 but every decode hangs). Reuses the #750 two-heartbeat oracle: the liveness pulse `fak_bgloop_up` (`internal/gateway/bgloops.go:227-234`) vs the decode-throughput `fak_gateway_inference_decode_tokens_per_second` (`metrics.go:1970-1975`, fall-to-0 :1968), RED when `/healthz`=up disagrees with decode-rate=0. Binds `*model.RecurrentEvictUnsupportedError` (`kvcache.go:54`) + `recoverRecurrentEvictUnsupported` (`gateway.go:1266`, wired into the deferred recover :1216) + the `deriveState` DARK fold (`loopmgr/health.go:281-312`, ticked-but-stale). Cross-check A (up vs throughput=0) + D (re-derive both from their metrics). Citations adversarially verified — the draft's `fak_bgloop_up` wrong-file (metrics.go→bgloops.go) and an `AdmittedTurns` over-claim (→ `fak_sched_admitted_total` `admission.go:507`) were caught and fixed. Renders C2 failure F1. Research/design only.
### Concepts, benchmarks & run logs

- [conceptbench findings — the adaptation loop's first turn (2026-07-24)](docs/notes/conceptbench-findings-2026-07-24.md) — the first conceptbench analysis (issue #2741, epic #2721): turns the rows the dos referee has graded so far into **one concrete fak change** ([#5380](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/5380), a tier-gated affordance hint echoing the `(fak <leaf>)` stamp + witness rule for weaker models). Scrupulously honest about the result-claim gate — as of 2026-07-24 every graded row is `source:"replay"`, so the honesty gate correctly pins `result_claim_allowed:false` and **no measured frontier-vs-small gap is claimed**; the replay-graded `commit_stamp` contrast (frontier `claude-opus-4-8` `OK`/`diff-witnessed` vs small `claude-3-5-haiku` `CLAIM_UNWITNESSED`/`stamp=none`) is used only to *localize* the small-model failure to a fak-affordance discoverability gap. The measured half is gated on a live gateway run (named promotion evidence), with demotion evidence and an invalidating assumption stated per the gen/next frame.
- [The agent programming grammar — lifting fak's invariants into a domain-free substrate (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-AGENT-PROGRAMMING-GRAMMAR-2026-06-28.md) — the "express + generalize" half of the innovations survey: fak's contribution is one invariant (*a decision no participant can move by narrating a number*) carried at every scale, not any single primitive (0/29 novel). Extracts that invariant as a reusable **grammar** — nouns (lane, lease, reason token, witness, verdict, claim, ladder rung, scope) and verbs (arbitrate, verify, audit, review, refuse, recall, resolve, status) — names the ~10 fak concepts already lifted into the domain-free DOS form, the two structural shapes a correct primitive obeys (the verification ladder; the security==optimization two lenses), and scopes the **under-expressed** concepts (`G1–G9`: readiness ladder, verification-ladder verb, durability-class promotion, materialized-view context contract, prediction-vs-reality calibration, one-sided-screen polarity, taint-check, per-aspect routing, net-true-value claim-check) as the next grammar verbs. The companion of the durable [innovations index](docs/INNOVATIONS-INDEX.md); actioned by the grammar epic.
- [The feature-maturity lifecycle ladder — completeness is not maturity, and every gap is the next work item (2026-06-29)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-MATURITY-LADDER-2026-06-29.md) — the **shipped** subsystem for the long-horizon "agentic culture" problem: a v1 prototype can be legitimately *complete* yet not tested, not dogfooded, not benchmarked, not the default — and the operator should SEE where each capability sits and what would mature it next. [`internal/maturity`](internal/maturity) places every declared fak capability (one per `internal/<leaf>` lane in [`dos.toml`](dos.toml) `[lanes.trees]`) on a closed lifecycle ladder — `proposed → prototyped → tested → dogfooded → default` (with a `benchmarked` badge) — each rung gated by evidence the author did not write (code on disk, a `*_test.go`, an edge in the running binary's transitive import graph, a documented verb), so a rung moves only by changing the real tree. **Immaturity is never a defect** (a complete v1 honestly sitting at `prototyped` is expected); the one thing it refuses is a **ladder-skip** — a capability fak relies on yet leaves untested (`maturity_debt`, the maturity sibling of the readiness ladder's `READINESS_OVERCLAIM`). For each capability the first unmet rung is rendered as a concrete **next work item**, and `fak maturity next` is the ranked backlog the issue-dispatch loop pulls from — the mechanization of "each agent has the desire to create the next ticket." The shipped fak-tree binding of grammar [G1 / the readiness ladder](docs/notes/CONCEPT-AGENT-PROGRAMMING-GRAMMAR-2026-06-28.md); first dogfood run over fak's own 109 lanes: index 78/100, 0 ladder-skips, 18 tested-but-not-dogfooded.
- [Complain by default — the agentic-dev friction channels and when to use each (2026-06-29)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-AGENT-FRICTION-COMPLAINT-CHANNELS-2026-06-29.md) — a playbook for the `/goal` "improve the default ability for agents to complain about annoying agentic-dev things": friction that no agent records stays trapped in per-agent private memory and is re-discovered by the next agent, so the default response to a papercut should be to register it through a structured, **deduplicating** channel that is part of the repo. Names a closed, evidence-grounded **friction taxonomy** (`shared-tree-clobber`, `red-trunk`, `stamp-refusal`, `push-blocked`, `saturated-box`, `tool-timeout`, `lane-collision`, `guard-false-positive`, `confusing-refusal`, `phantom-artifact`) and routes each to the channel that fits *who acts and how fast* — operator/status surfacing via `fak blockers post|feed` (shipped), the capability-floor **appeal** that must carry the witnessed journal verdict (`internal/guardcomplaint`; the `fak complain` dispatch is `not yet` wired — mid-rework on the shared tree), the DOS closed **refusal vocabulary** (`dos_refuse_reasons`/`dos_check_reason`) to decline green with a verifiable reason, and the `internal/dogfoodissues` dedup/escalate fold for recurring process friction. Four rules for complaining as signal not noise (structured+deduped, carry a witness not a feeling, name the recovery you wanted, route by who acts). Companion of the [agent programming grammar](docs/notes/CONCEPT-AGENT-PROGRAMMING-GRAMMAR-2026-06-28.md); the `not yet` half (wire the verb + a `workflow`/`dev` domain) is the next checkable step.
- [Local inference platforms — source-dated 64/128 GB decision inputs (#9573) (2026-08-27)](docs/notes/LOCAL-INFERENCE-PLATFORMS-2026-08-27.md) — AMD Strix Halo first, then representative Apple unified-memory, NVIDIA compact-appliance, and CPU/high-memory hosts. Separates marketed capacity from conservative usable inference memory, official specifications from measured evidence, and hardware inputs from not-yet-measured fak-native Qwen3.8/GLM-5.3 performance.
- [Long-context stacked-machine topology economics (#9576) (2026-08-28)](docs/notes/LONG-CONTEXT-TOPOLOGY-MODEL-2026-08-28.md) — bounded 1/2/4-node and 10/25/100 GbE accounting for replication, shared-prefix routing, and model-parallel execution; compares only quality-qualified completed-job cost and time.
- [Affordable-hardware agent fleets — the doable tier, and what shows it (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/AFFORDABLE-HARDWARE-AGENT-FLEETS-2026-06-28.md) — a strategy/framing + showable-demo map for running **multiple** coding agents **safely** on hardware a small team can afford (one big-RAM CPU box, one or a few older datacenter GPU/Ampere GPUs, a gaming GPU, or a Mac), including overnight/offline. Thesis: the agent fleet is the workload where affordable hardware wins, because it is latency-tolerant (batch it overnight), prefix-heavy (pay the setup once), and unattended (so trust, not tok/s, binds). Separates the **witnessed-today** tier (sub-frontier ≤27B — the 27B @64–128-concurrency curve, the 4070 862-agg-tok/s batch, the Mac 4.1× fleet stack, the 1,024-agent CPU `fanrun`, the GPU-free `fleet-reuse-demo`) from the **honest not-yet** (frontier 753B GLM-5.2 on CPU-offload at sub-1 tok/s — the #971 host-expert wall; TPU = no backend), and ties each tier to the specific fak lever (RadixAttention prefix reuse, continuous batching, resident-Q4_K, capacity preflight) and safety rail (default-deny capabilities, `dos_arbitrate` disjoint lanes, commit-audit, quarantine) that makes it usable. Every measured number cited to its authority row; every projection labeled.
- [Desktop slowness maintenance runbook — sprawl, not resources, on the fleet workstation (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/DESKTOP-SLOWNESS-MAINTENANCE-2026-06-28.md) — why the workstation "feels slow" is not resource exhaustion (CPU ~32%, RAM 25% used, disk queue 0, no thermal throttle) but two reboot-cleared accumulations: one WindowsTerminal rendering all fleet panes (2.2 GB / 14.7k handles) and a growing TermService/RDP handle leak (2.5 → 3.0 GB within a single session). Records the reboot cadence, the Defender build-tree exclusion command, and the verified do-NOT list (do not bulk-kill "idle" python/pwsh — they are live DOS-MCP/session workers, classified by parent liveness; do not clear the shared ~18 GB go-build cache).
- [D-006 (#243) Tool Ecosystem Expansion — architecture-fit decision + honest status (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/D-006-TOOL-ECOSYSTEM-ARCH-FIT-243-2026-06-28.md) — discharges the [#304 tracker](docs/notes/track-d-agent-framework-parity-tracking-304.md)'s deferred "decide the architecture fit first" step for [#243](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/243): fak's tool ecosystem is **gate-native, not provider-native**. Decides it on on-disk evidence — fak already ships the confined, kernel-routed `fak_read` built-in (#795) plus the `readOnly`/`idempotent`/`destructive` safety-annotation layer (`metaFor`) — so the tracker's "🔴 Not implemented" D-006 row is now 🟡 partial (read + annotation layer shipped; glob/write/HTTP/DB built-ins not). Reframes the LangChain-shaped acceptance, names the single open gate (the unbuilt boxes + the 20-tool target), and scopes the smallest next leaf (`fak_glob`: read-only, confined, mirrors `fak_read`). Honest: a decision note does not tick the feature boxes, so #243's literal acceptance stays open pending re-scope or the multi-day build.
- [fak guard SECRET_EXFIL audit — banner clarity + version build-provenance (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/AUDIT-fak-guard-secret-exfil-2026-06-28.md) — the audit behind 06179a0c: the SECRET_EXFIL quarantine banner is current HEAD behavior (default-on normgate), not a stale binary; `fak version` now stamps the binary's VCS revision so a stale binary is detectable; the inbound-quarantine banner was rewritten to plain-language, non-alarming text. Documents the over-firing detector (witnessed on the kernel's own source) and the more-permissive-detection options, leaving the tested secrets-are-absolute-by-source stance as an explicit maintainer decision.
- [Two cache stacks, one wire — fak's cache concepts vs the agent harness's, and do we disable Claude Code auto-compaction? (#1131) (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/HARNESS-CACHE-COHERENCE-AUDIT-2026-06-28.md) — audit of the coherence between fak's cache/context machinery (cache-preserving compaction #745, budget reset / cut-vs-reset #774, prompt-MMU #751, ctxmmu quarantine, paged-KV evict, cachemeta prefix-stability/coherence) and the agent harness's OWN machinery (Claude Code auto-compaction, `cache_control` prompt-cache, ~5-min TTL). Names the interference matrix: the two managers stack blind to each other — fak is cache-**preserving**, the harness's auto-compaction is cache-**destroying** (it rewrites the `cache_control` prefix, bursting the cache fak just preserved) and can defeat a quarantine by folding a sealed span into its summary. Answers the explicit question with evidence: Claude Code auto-compaction **cannot be reliably disabled via settings/env** (the toggle is silently ignored; threshold clamped ~83%; anthropics/claude-code #38483/#42817/#42149) — the one dependable lever is a **`PreCompact` hook (exit 2)**, so the answer is a conditional protocol (**block** while fak copes, **allow** when fak's compaction bails, **reset** past TTL), not a flag flip. Lands the spine [`internal/compactcohere`](internal/compactcohere/compactcohere.go): a pure tier-1 sensor that attributes a per-turn prefix event (`stable`/`fak_cut`/`fak_world_break`/`harness_rewrite`/`cold_ttl`) + a standing block/allow PreCompact posture, reusing `cachemeta` divergence rather than duplicating it. Actuators (gateway wiring, the guard hook, the quarantine-survival witness) are the epic.
- [Ultracode dogfood — a 9-agent fleet advancing Qwen3.6 parity (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/ULTRACODE-DOGFOOD-QWEN36-PARITY-2026-06-28.md) — a measured session record of fak's *ultracode* (multi-agent) mode run as two concurrent waves (4 production + 5 verification agents on disjoint file lanes) against the Qwen3.6-27B-vs-llama.cpp parity goal: 8 independent reviewed deliverables, ~48 min of agent labor compressed into ~20 min agent-phase wall-clock (**2.38× wall-clock / 8× deliverable-count**), with the agent-orchestration multiple fenced strictly apart from fak's separate **inference** 5–10x (different axis, cited never blended). Links the parity rollup ([`QWEN36-PARITY-ROLLUP-2026-06-28`](docs/notes/QWEN36-PARITY-ROLLUP-2026-06-28.md)), the [token-3 drift investigation](experiments/qwen36/token3-drift-investigation-2026-06-28.md), the [one-command Mac gate](tools/qwen36_mac_parity_gate.sh), the [ultracode-mode explainer](docs/explainers/ultracode-multi-agent-dogfood.md), and the [machine-readable witness](experiments/agent-live/ultracode-dogfood-witness-20260628.json).
- [Evict-on-paged KV — the bit-exact middle-span Evict survives a block-paged layout (#33) (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/EVICT-ON-PAGED-KV-DESIGN-2026-06-28.md) — design + prototype proof for [#33](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/33) (the gate before the native paged allocator [#34](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/34) and the L3 governance referee [#27](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/27)). Verdict **GO**: fak's kernel-owned, bit-exact middle-span `KVCache.Evict` carries forward onto a block-paged layout because the single-rotation re-RoPE of a shifted survivor depends only on its pre-RoPE `Kraw` and its **new logical position**, not on physical contiguity. Two requirements proven on real float32 KV with no GPU: the paged blocks must carry a per-token `Kraw` plane (the shipped #277 allocator stored only K/V — `NewPagedKVPoolWithRaw` adds it), and a survivor re-RoPE must copy-on-write-split any shared block before mutating K. A mid-span evict on a deliberately non-contiguous page table is **byte-for-byte identical** to the contiguous `Evict` (cache **and** next-token logits, `max|Δ|=0` on amd64), and a COW-forked sibling is left untouched (`internal/model/paged_evict.go`, `internal/model/paged_evict_test.go`). Names the honest cost (an intra-block compaction on middle-span evict, which vLLM/SGLang avoid only because they cannot evict a middle span at all), the FP8/INT8-KV decision (keep `Kraw` f32 per [#1047](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/1047); defer quantized-K-with-exact-span), and scopes the GLM-DSA path out with reason (its `glm.rerotateSurvivor` follows the identical new-logical-index re-derivation; carrying the DSA state onto blocks is #34's job).
- [`L3RegionBackend` — child B of epic #79, designed behind the frozen `Ref`/`Resolver`/`RegionBackend` seam (#55) (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/L3REGIONBACKEND-CHILD-B-DESIGN-55-2026-06-28.md) — the `design(` artifact for [#55](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/55) (Option B): the interface, span→page-key contract, resolve/page-in/page-out/evict lifecycle, G2 middle-eviction fold, and G3 deletion-certificate tier-projection that unlock **G2** (evict a poisoned span from the *middle* of pages shared across sequences) and native **G3** (attest the *L3 pages* were destroyed, not just the local copy) — neither reachable from a control-path-only sidecar. Specifies `L3RegionBackend` as a **backend swap** under the frozen `abi.RegionBackend` seam (`internal/abi/registry.go:609`) with **no ABI edit**, each method named against the external store's `mget`/`mset`/evict, the per-page `Ref.Digest` verify stated as a **fail-closed** page-in admission gate (G1), `evict(from,n)` mapped through fak-owned `KVCache.Evict` re-rotation (bit-exact `max|Δ|=0` survivors) to an L3 page-key delete + re-key, and `deletioncert.Mint` folded to attest the L3 page-key set offline-verifiably. Honest spine: the in-kernel engine (v0.2) OR a co-residence handshake is a **HARD [GAP] ~120h-class precondition**, the design stays on the **control path** (§6.5: opt-in admission verify, not an inline per-RDMA-read byte-scan; ~1–5 µs L3 read budget named), the staged plan gates run on a **mock/local** L3 (real-store wire optional/follow-on), it composes with [#493](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/493) (network `StageTransport`) for the multi-node case, and the whole backend is **gated behind Option A** (the referee sidecar) proving the seam first. The design row [`L3-DISAGGREGATED-CACHE-REIMAGINED`](docs/notes/L3-DISAGGREGATED-CACHE-REIMAGINED.md) (epic [#79](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/79)) labelled "child B".
- [Frontier agent leaderboard survey (#1068) (2026-06-27)](docs/notes/AGENTIC-LEADERBOARD-SURVEY-1068-2026-06-27.md) — surveys the frontier agent / tool-use leaderboards to choose the external benchmark fak measures itself against for [#1068](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/1068); recommendation: BFCL V4.
- [Combinatorial-growth epic — a stable core as models × backends × features multiply (#1079) (2026-06-27)](docs/notes/COMBINATORIAL-GROWTH-EPIC-2026-06-27.md) — the decomposition for [#1079](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/1079): fak's correctness cross-product (16 model families × 4 backends × the perf-feature set) is tracked by hand-written prose that rots (#487's grid opens with "the epic thesis is stale") and by oracle tests that SKIP in CI, leaving 12 `requirePreNorm` panic cells and 87 boundary markers invisible. Frames five children that make growth legible and gated without a core edit or a brittle weight-backed test: C1 #1080 (generate the support matrix FROM the tree + a `growth_debt` metric in the existing control-pane), C2 #1081 (a weight-free per-family conformance contract that actually runs in CI), C3 #1082 (`fak new-model` leaf scaffold), C4 #1083 (a researcher experiment registry), C5 #1084 (the growth-gardening cadence + the stable-core ritual). Distinct from `steerability` (structure), `industry-scorecard`/#1050 (the field), and #487 (building the seam) — this owns the seam between them.
- [Concept-popularization epic — make the ideas known and attractive (2026-07-02)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-POPULARIZATION-EPIC-2026-07-02.md) — the decomposition for making fak's five core concepts (treat the tool call like a syscall; verify-don't-trust / DOS; the addressable bit-exact KV cache; reviewable tool-call control plus result quarantine; the single static Go binary drop-in) genuinely popular, not just findable. Complements the AEO/SEO mechanism ([AEO next steps](docs/notes/AEO-MARKETING-NEXT-STEPS-2026-07-01.md)) with the human-facing half: 50 self-contained tickets (filed under the `popularization` label) across eleven dimensions — explainer content (×8), visual/diagram assets (×5), interactive demos (×6), positioning/comparison (×5), social proof/community (×5), developer onramp (×5), integration recipes (×4), benchmark-as-story (×4), memorable framing (×3), distribution (×3), and an adoption-measurement scorecard (×2). Every ticket carries a checkable acceptance and an honest-scope fence (no market-adoption claim, no unrun benchmark, no novelty the 0/29 prior-art audit refutes).
- [score-signal autonomous live-filing posture (#980) (2026-06-27)](docs/notes/SCORE-SIGNAL-AUTO-ARM-2026-06-27.md) — the operator decision (full flip to `--live` on the daily schedule) that makes the score-signal feeder file tickets without a human, and why it is safe: the storm bound is the feeder's own label-scoped dedup + worst-first cap + refresh-not-re-file (#981), not a human in the loop.
- [Dojo `cross_session_warm_hit_rate` is bimodal by workload (#963) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/DOJO-WARM-HIT-BIMODAL-963-2026-06-26.md) — resolution note for [#963](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/963): the `fak dojo` resume-posture lever's warm-hit claim (a fixed 0.17) is bimodal by workload — realized 0.65 on a long-running dev tree (`C--work-fak`, UNDER_CLAIM) and ~0.00 on batch corpora (`C--work-job`, `C--work-Benchmark`, OVER_CLAIM), while the sibling `cold_write_share`/`posture_accuracy` claims calibrate fine. Records the shipped increment (claim recalibrated 0.17 → 0.0 as the conservative never-over-claim lower bound in `f306cfb9`/`265ce039`, plus the duplicate-`registerDojoLevers` build fix in `76bc0806`) and the deliberately deferred follow-on (option 2: report `cross_session_warm_hit_rate` as an OBSERVED spread excluded from the calibration grade, which needs an `internal/dojo` report-only verdict plus `cmd/fak/dojo.go` wiring).

- [Verified resume packet — sourcing the checkpoint from witnessed state (#636) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/VERIFIED-RESUME-PACKET-CHECKPOINT-2026-06-26.md) — design note for [#636](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/636): make `tools/session_checkpoint.py` a **verified, resumable packet** instead of a self-reported git-snapshot note. Sketches the `fak-session-checkpoint/2` schema that demotes the free-text `in_flight` line to a fallback and adds a `witnessed` block (the `dos_status` `{liveness, progress, region, resume}` digest — which has no `claimed` field by construction — plus the last *N* `trajectory.Turn` verdict rows for the active trace) and an agent-facing `resume` block. Specifies the fail-soft consume path (`--witnessed --run-id … [--corpus …]`, degrading cleanly to v1) and the exact three things a fresh/cloud session needs to continue (region = held lease, last witnessed phase = ledger-VERIFIED rung + verdict tail, context pointer = the transcript path today / a digest-addressed `abi.RegisterKVBackend` KV span next). Names the flag-gated code fold as the `tools`-lane follow-on (per `AGENTS.md`, its end-state is a Go `fak checkpoint` with the Python script as reference consumer).
- [Agent-OS scheduling SOTA readout: lease, heartbeat, fence, schedule, autoscale (#906) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/ORCHESTRATION-LEASE-HEARTBEAT-SCHEDULE-SOTA-2026-06-26.md) — research/SOTA readout for [#906](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/906): maps Kubernetes Lease + leaderelection, Temporal heartbeats, Argo Workflows synchronization, and Ray Serve autoscaling against fak's current loopmgr/lease/heartbeat seams with file:line citations, keeping the five decisions distinct (heartbeat = liveness, lease = admission, fence = stale-write rejection, schedule = overlap policy, autoscale = fair reallocation). Answers the issue's five questions from the tree (`leaseref.Record` is the cross-machine lease object — owner/acquired-at/TTL/target, no renew or fencing token; the witness rung grades *done* not *alive*; the governor is cadence/back-pressure not an overlap policy) and names the concrete gaps as children: no fencing/generation token on `leaseref.Record`, a defined-but-unproduced loop `heartbeat` event ([#750](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/750)), and no skip/queue/coalesce/replace overlap policy distinct from the cadence floor.
- [Backend SOTA matrix: where fak should borrow, bind, or stay minimal (#905) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-backend-sota-matrix-2026-06-26.md) — inward engineering map for [#905](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/905): for each compute operation fak's kernel actually performs, records the current fak path (verified against the tree), the production kernel stack to learn from (CUTLASS/FlashInfer/TRT-LLM/Marlin/AWQ), the candidate integration route (borrow/bind/stay minimal), the verification oracle, and the expected bottleneck. Selects the AWQ CUDA witness gap as the falsifiable next optimization target; honest about the one gate this environment cannot reach. **Promoted into the maintained, load-bearing [SOTA prior-art matrix](docs/sota/README.md) — read that first; this note is the dated origin.**
- [Structured-output decoding for schema-valid tool-call candidates (#907) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-structured-output-decoding-2026-06-26.md) — guided-decoding SOTA readout + field map for [#907](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/907): the current structured-output engines (vLLM/SGLang `response_format`/`guided_*`/`json_schema`, XGrammar/llguidance/Outlines as the mask backends) with docs linked, and a field map from every constraint surface (OpenAI `response_format`/`logit_bias`, vLLM/SGLang guided fields, Anthropic/MCP tool schema, fak `grammar.Grammar`) onto **one canonical internal shape** — `grammar.Grammar` → `oneOf` JSON Schema → the `response_format` carrier (ride mode) or a logit mask (native). Wires the **ride-mode passthrough** the issue asked for: the OpenAI proxy now forwards `response_format`/`logit_bias` to the ride engine verbatim and adjudicates the constrained candidate after generation (`internal/gateway/structured_output_passthrough_test.go`, GPU-free, bit-exact-off when absent). Native sampler (logit-bias first, schema mask flagged, bit-exact-off acceptance) is filed as [#929](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/929); builds on [#469](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/469)/[#560](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/560).
- [Resume & rehome runbook — restarting rate-limit-crashed Claude sessions (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESUME-REHOME-RUNBOOK-2026-06-26.md) — the durable procedure + tool index for restarting rate-limit-crashed Claude Code sessions: the four-stage pipeline (`resume_sweep` discover → `fleet_sessions registry --probe` plan → `fleet_resume_watchdog --live` launch → `resume_relaunch_audit` verify), the two gates that make a fresh account an admissible rehome target (a `projects/` marker + a live probe for positive-evidence — the day26 lesson), why the "organization has disabled subscription access" banner is the token-twin smear and not org policy, the inter-launch-spacing fix (`FAK_LAUNCH_SPACING_SEC`) that stops a resume burst self-congesting on a transient 529, and the failure-class disposition (API_ERR self-heals, LIMIT defers to reset, AUTH needs login/rehome). Names the Go port (`fak resume`) as the next step — the tools are frozen Python. — inward engineering map for issue #905: for each compute operation fak actually performs, records the current path, production kernel stack to learn from, integration route, verification oracle, and expected bottleneck; selects the AWQ CUDA witness gap as a falsifiable next optimization target without claiming it shipped.
- [The top unscored surfaces — a scorecard-coverage gap map (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/SCORECARD-COVERAGE-GAPS-2026-06-26.md) — backlog of high-value deterministic scorecards not yet folded into the control pane, anchored by the shipped skill-effectiveness scorecard and ranked across type contracts, refusal taxonomy, benchmark catalog, witness completeness, integration coverage, lint coverage, SLAs, metrics, examples, and migrations.
- [Agentic dev observability score — current read (2026-07-01)](docs/notes/AGENTIC-DEV-OBSERVABILITY-SCORE-2026-07-01.md) — current score read for fak's own agentic-development process: gateway observability is `96.8/A` with zero hard debt, agent readiness is `100.0/A`, the full control pane is still `scorecard_unmeasured` because dirty-tree Go compile blockers fan out across Go-backed scorecards, and follow-up tickets #1997, #1998, and #2043 carry the remaining gaps.
- [Durable session state → shared memory: use cases, SOTA, and the kill-safe session (2026-07-01)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-DURABLE-SESSION-STATE-SHARED-MEMORY-2026-07-01.md) — research + design note on making long-session state durable instead of window-resident: names the operator's three feelings (brittleness, "almost there", "losing the context") as one structural fact — state whose only copy is a context window is an unmeasured liability. Surveys mid-2026 SOTA across durable execution (Temporal/DBOS/Restate record-once-never-reask journaling), agent memory products (distilled facts, background consolidation, index-over-detail; staleness and multi-writer handling still open field-wide), and documented practitioner pain (compaction horror stories, context rot, handoff-file folk remedies). Maps fak's shipped pieces (session_checkpoint floor, sessionimage/snapshot, sessionreset carryover, ctxplan/recall store, ctxmmu truth-duration gate, memview provenance, dos_status/dos_recall/dos_arbitrate) and finds every weakness is a missing *composition*, not a missing primitive — fak already ships the field's two "nobody does this" items. Proposes the ladder: P1 turn-boundary witnessed journal (extends [#636](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/636)), P2 residual meter + `KILL_SAFE` verdict (measure *unrecorded* window state, not window fullness), P3 restart-parity probe (a referee that grades "this checkpoint suffices to resume" instead of trusting it), P4 witnessed promotion gate into shared memory (truth-duration + provenance + recall-time re-verification end to end), P5 memory tree as an arbitrated dos lane, P6 heal the forked store. Each rung carries its next checkable step; §7 fences the model-relative residual definition and the shallow-parity failure mode.
- [Typed virtual views with provenance gates — the memory-view contract (#904) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/MEMORY-VIEW-CONTRACT-2026-06-26.md) — research/SOTA readout + design note for issue #904: a memory/context cell is CANONICAL (the raw bytes), and every summary/QA/graph/prompt-prefix/KV-prefix projection is a DERIVED `MemoryViewRecord` (`internal/memview`) that binds to its source by a sha256 digest + byte span, inherits the source taint, carries an `abi.Verdict` admission gate, and re-enters adjudication before any effect. Surveys seven primary sources (MemGPT/MemOS/Mem0/GraphRAG/LightRAG/HippoRAG/A-MEM), maps the prompt-MMU/recall/vcachechain/ctxplan/ctxmmu leaves onto the view table, and fences exact lossless prompt/KV reuse apart from lossy semantic views. One lossless view (snippet) is materialized from raw bytes and invalidated on a source-digest change (test-witnessed green).
- [Memory-view formats — a third, orthogonal surface/ablate axis (2026-07-03)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-MEMORY-VIEW-FORMATS-2026-07-03.md) — extends the [memory-view contract](docs/notes/MEMORY-VIEW-CONTRACT-2026-06-26.md) with a Format axis (`internal/memview/format.go`): once a view is admitted and rendered, HOW it is serialized (markdown / JSON / a fak-local flat TOON subset) is a separate, open-registry, deterministic choice, measured (not assumed) via `SweepFormats` — a same-content byte/estimated-token ablation across formats (test-witnessed: TOON strictly cheaper than JSON for uniform rows). First consumed by `fak memory recall --format` / `--list-formats` / `--ablate-formats`, with a withheld note's body still never crossing under any format.
- [Coding as a primary use case — scorecard + next-steps spine (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/CODING-USE-CASE-SCORECARD-2026-06-26.md) — steps through all 29 examples from a coding-agent lens (the floors are solid; the demos are mostly generic-capability proofs — no re-runnable "coding agent behind `fak guard`" demo yet, the open #318/#344/#321/#632 cluster) and grades the use case across five dimensions: **adopt A−** (`fak guard -- claude` one command, 38/44 harnesses repointable), **floor B+** (deny-by-structure gitgate + self-modify globs, proven by tests not a demo), **cost C+** (cache-preserving compaction dogfooded at 95% shed / byte-identical prefix but on a **mock** upstream — cascade-on-real-traffic unverified, [#745](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/745)), **witness D+** (zero measured raw-vs-fak coding result; SWE-bench `pass_rate=0` GPU-gated; Terminal-Bench all contract-phase, [#897](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/897)), **in-kernel B−** (argmax-exact forward shipped, no coding instance solved through it, [#610](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/610)). Maps the ordered spine (witness → cost → make-floor-visible → finish-adopt → in-kernel) onto the live epics and names the one unowned gap: no umbrella epic frames "coding agent behind fak" as the primary use case.
- [Decisions audit log: git-notes readback for adjudication records (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/DECISIONS-AUDIT-LOG-2026-06-26.md) — reader path for [#833](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/833): `witness.Decision` JSON-lines notes on `refs/notes/fak/decisions`, with `git notes --ref=refs/notes/fak/decisions show <sha>` for commit-anchored records and the empty-tree sentinel for pre-commit refusals. Documents that the ref stays off `main`, does not change `git log main`, and needs explicit fetch/push.
- [Witness attestation signing-key decision: operator-supplied Ed25519 first (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/WITNESS-ATTESTATION-SIGNING-DECISION-2026-06-26.md) — signer decision for [#832](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/832): v0 portable witness attestations should use an operator-supplied Ed25519 DSSE key so the first token stays zero-dependency and offline-capable; Sigstore/gitsign remains a later identity upgrade once the fleet has an OIDC/root policy. Pins the in-toto Statement / DSSE envelope shape and repeats the honest scope: the SHA is already content-addressed; the additive guarantee is the signed identity over fak's local witness verdict.
- [The readiness / durability verdict ladder as a domain-free DOS primitive (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/CONCEPT-DOS-READINESS-VERDICT-LADDER-2026-06-26.md) — concept capture for [#582](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/582): the mechanism behind the product scorecard ([`tools/product_scorecard.py`](tools/product_scorecard.py) / [PRODUCT-STATUS](docs/PRODUCT-STATUS.md)) is not fak-specific — it is a generic DOS primitive. A **closed maturity ladder** (`durable-product → … → concept-only`) where every rung's promotion is gated by **evidence the claimant did not author** (the CLAIMS tag a concept carries, whether its first command resolves to a real `cmd/` dir + documented verb, whether its witness/entry docs exist on disk), plus a **surface-ceiling gate** that stops a benchmark from posing as a durable product and a stub from posing as shipped — so a verdict can only move by changing the real tree, never the data. The note (1) extracts the surface-gate + evidence-binding core into a domain-free five-part form (closed rung vocabulary, surface→ceiling cap, capability set, evidence predicates over ground truth, evidence-bound adjudication with overclaim-refusal), (2) sketches a `dos`-grounded variant where the top rung is closed by a `dos` **witness** (`dos_verify` reads git ancestry, `dos_commit_audit` separates a `diff-witnessed` claim from a forgeable `subject-only` one) and an overclaim is refused with a structured reason from a closed vocabulary (cf. `dos_refuse_reasons`, proposed token `READINESS_OVERCLAIM`), and (3) decides where it lives: **keep the tree-cross-check shell a fak tool, lift the closed-ladder + ceiling-gate + evidence-bound-adjudication + refusal core into the DOS concept vocabulary** — building a `dos readiness` verb is a dos-kernel follow-up named as the next step, not committed here (the issue scoped this to concept capture, no code).
- [One machine: the session lifecycle IS serving admission (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/SESSION-LIFECYCLE-IS-SERVING-ADMISSION-2026-06-26.md) — explores the parallel between session start/resume and dynamic fused serving of random-length, flexible-turn sessions. The thesis: the `internal/session` run-state machine (start/throttle/pause/drain/stop + cold `Restore` + `Recontinue`) and the continuous-batch serving operation (admit/preempt/evict/resume a sequence) are **one state machine at two altitudes**, with the agent **turn boundary** as the single admission/preemption/resume quantum. fak already implies this but spells the machine twice (`session.RunState` vs `loopmgr.LoopState`, two enums sharing `paused/draining/stopped`) and only advisory-couples the halves (`TurnIntent` is consumed by prewarm/discard admission but gates no live served batch). Maps the verified correspondence (session verb ↔ serving verb ↔ status ↔ grounding symbol), separates the buildable seams (shared vocabulary, one epoch lineage, `SlotEvent`→KV reclaim, warm-swap on resume) from the analogy-only rows (cross-host live migration; `KVIncluded=false` by design), positions against prior art ([#805](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/805)/[#748](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/748)/[#637](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/637)/[#809](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/809)/[#844](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/844)), and proposes the unification epic [#912](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/912) (children [#913](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/913)–[#916](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/916)). Decomposition adversarially verified by a multi-agent workflow; every cited file:symbol and issue state confirmed against the tree.
- [Is witnessed span-attention a valid reward? Verdict, aggregation, and failure modes (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-witnessed-span-attention-validity-2026-06-26.md) — prior-art survey 1/5 for the reward-over-spans epic [#860](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/860): the attention-as-explanation debate resolved into a usable rule for [#861](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/861). Verdict: witnessed span-attention is a cheap, relative, span-level proposal signal, not a standalone causal explanation or accepted reward until it correlates with exact-eviction leave-one-out. The note maps Jain/Wallace, Wiegreffe/Pinter, Serrano/Smith, and Abnar/Zuidema to fak's actual post-softmax observer and span ledger, then pins the aggregation rule: consumer rows only, span-level mass, all observed heads/layers summed for direct cache-read accounting, position/length normalization, and top/bottom LOO validation before any live planner or RSI promotion. Satisfies [#861](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/861).
- [Span-attention confound normalization: sinks, position, recency, and length (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-span-attention-confound-normalization-2026-06-26.md) — prior-art survey 2/5 for the reward-over-spans epic [#860](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/860): the normalization contract for [#862](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/862). Raw attention is demoted to telemetry unless `expected_attention_mass` subtracts sink/preamble positions, massive-activation/outlier token effects, span length, relative-position/U-shaped long-context bias, and distance-to-query/recency defaults. The note defines the null-model rungs (uniform-visible fallback, position-length baseline, distance/age baseline, sink mask, per-head/layer calibration) and maps them to `SpanRewardSegment.ExpectedAttentionMass`, `NormalizedReward`, and `ConfoundNormalizationImproved`, preserving the rule that exact-eviction LOO decides whether the residual attention actually mattered. Satisfies [#862](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/862).
- [Span-reward novelty boundary: H2O, ContextCite, PRMs, and the defensible claim (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-span-reward-novelty-boundary-2026-06-26.md) — prior-art synthesis 5/5 for the reward-over-spans epic [#860](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/860): the honest novelty boundary against H2O/SnapKV/Quest, ContextCite, influence methods, and PRMs. The defensible claim is a witnessed, replayable, falsifiable span-credit proxy, not "attention proves causality"; the note lists the exact fak deltas, the H2O-with-new-names dimensions, and the forbidden overclaims downstream docs must avoid. Satisfies [#865](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/865).
- [Span-credit signal menu: attention, gradients, leave-one-out, influence, and the fak fallback (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-span-credit-signal-menu-2026-06-26.md) — prior-art survey 3/5 for the reward-over-spans epic [#860](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/860): ranks candidate context-span credit signals by faithfulness times cost, with the recommended design for [#863](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/863): attention is a cheap proposal distribution, exact-eviction leave-one-out verifies the top/bottom candidates, and `CORRELATE` / `REFUTE` / `INSUFFICIENT` decides whether attention may be promoted, replaced by LOO deltas, or held for more evidence. The note maps Integrated Gradients, Grad x Input, occlusion, influence functions, TracIn, ContextCite, and the attention-explanation debate onto fak's shipped `span_reward_shadow` and `ctxplan` seams, while preserving the overclaim fence that no live planner/RSI flip happens until #866 proves correlation against the eviction witness. Satisfies [#863](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/863).
- [Mac Qwen3.6-27B Metal Q4_K perf diagnosis: decode 1.2 / prefill 0.6 tok/s, the per-call command-buffer wall (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/MAC-QWEN36-27B-Q4K-METAL-PERF-DIAGNOSIS-2026-06-26.md) — a clean on-device measurement (M3 Pro, `-tags fakmetal`, `FAK_Q4K=1 FAK_METAL=1`, llama-server stopped) of fak's resident-Q4_K Metal path for the big Mac model, toward the 3× decode goal. The kernels are **bit-correct** (q4k GEMV cosine 1.000000, greedy token-parity vs CPU, both green on-box) but throughput is **0.16× of SOTA**: decode 1.2 tok/s vs llama.cpp-Metal 7.29, prefill 0.6 vs 51.55. `FAK_QPROFILE` phase profiles + the `q4k_gemv`/`q4k_gemm` microbench locate the wall precisely — each decode token issues ~336 *separate* Metal command-buffer GEMVs (≈7 matmuls × ~48–64 layers) and an isolated GEMV is **~360 µs launch/sync-bound** on top of ~98 µs of actual work (GEMV 32 GB/s ≈21% bandwidth; GEMM 364 GFLOP/s ≈5% FLOP). Re-prioritizes epic [#59](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/59): the kernel children (#515 q4_k GEMM / #516 resident weights / #517 decode GEMV / #71 hybrid prefill) have effectively **landed**, so the binding levers are now [#67](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/67) (one MTLCommandBuffer per forward — the GPU-resident decode forward, decode twin of the shipped `mg_prefill`) and a kernel-efficiency pass. Witness: `experiments/benchmark/runs/by-machine/node-macos-a/20260626T055239Z-q4k-metal-decode-27b/score.json`.
- [Mac Qwen2.5-7B Q8 GPU-resident decode forward: at performant parity with llama.cpp-Metal (0.99×) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/MAC-QWEN25-7B-Q8-RESIDENT-DECODE-2026-06-26.md) — the [#67](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/67) one-command-buffer GPU-resident **dense Q8 decode forward** (the decode twin of the shipped `mg_prefill`), built, optimized and measured on the M3 Pro. **Correct** — logit cosine **0.999991** + greedy token-parity vs the CPU Q8 decode (`TestMetalDecodeResidentMatchesCPU`), and the real Qwen2.5-1.5B Q8 emits the same text as CPU (`"...France is" -> "Paris"`); engages on Qwen2.5-7B Q8 with attention bias. **Six shipped optimizations took clean 7B Q8 decode 10.0 -> 17.6 tok/s (0.56× -> 0.988× of llama.cpp-Metal 17.82) — AT PARITY** (within ~1.2%, inside run-to-run variance): a **parallel RMSNorm** (the v0 ran the H-wide norm on ONE GPU thread ×56/token — a matmul-only split found it; +0.24×), the **LM head + final norm on the GPU** (+0.08×), **f16 block scales** + fused bias (GGUF Q8_0 std, ~6% fewer bytes; +0.04×), **persistent on-GPU KV** (seed once, append in place; +0.025×), and **key-parallel flash-decode attention** (8 simdgroups/head split the keys + flash-combine — a skip-attention split isolated the serial O(ctx) attention as the last residual; +0.04×). fak's projection GEMVs already run at **~154 GB/s, ABOVE llama's ~143**, and the matmul+head floor (~55.5 ms) is below llama's full decode (~56.1 ms). Shipped `5a11a31`..`d6188182`. Continues [MAC-QWEN36-27B-Q4K-METAL-PERF-DIAGNOSIS](docs/notes/MAC-QWEN36-27B-Q4K-METAL-PERF-DIAGNOSIS-2026-06-26.md).
- [idea-scout triage: out-of-band injection-defense taxonomy + adaptive evaluation (#909) (2026-06-26)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-out-of-band-injection-defense-taxonomy-triage-2026-06-26.md) — triage of arXiv:2606.26479 (Narisetty/Kore/Kattamanchi/Kumarapu, "Adaptive Evaluation of Out-of-Band Defenses Against Prompt Injection in LLM Agents"): the **survey that names fak's own family** — defend a tool-using agent against indirect prompt injection NOT by training the model to refuse but by enforcing security *outside* the model with a deterministic policy that mediates its actions (CaMeL/FIDES/Progent/RTBAS/FORGE), organized as classical **reference monitoring + Biba integrity + least privilege**. Verdict: **prior art to cite** — fak IS an out-of-band defense (default-deny capability floor = reference monitor + least privilege, IFC `Ref.Taint` sink-gating = Biba integrity, result-admit quarantine = containment that holds when the model is fooled), so the taxonomy is the academic name for the seams [`docs/industry-scorecard/security.md`](docs/industry-scorecard/security.md) already calls "FIDES/CaMeL-class". The paper's second contribution — every one of these defenses is validated only on **static** benchmarks, the same methodology that made in-band defenses look strong until adaptive attacks broke twelve at >90% — independently backs fak's deliberate non-load-bearing-detection bet (~100% evadable, by design) while **raising the bar** fak's own `internal/agentdojo` ASR battery should meet (adaptive, defense-aware, not static); the paper's own adaptive result is one small black-box data point (Progent on AgentDojo, Qwen2.5-7B/H200, mean ASR 25.8%→4.2%, adaptive 2.6%, white-box GCG still open), cited as a protocol to adopt for evaluation, never as evidence fak's containment is adaptively robust. No capability adopted (fak already enforces out-of-band); not a threat (a parallel defense on fak's side of the trust boundary). Resolved by `e7a6607`.
- [Compaction vs. the industry: drop-and-splice vs summarize-and-resend (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/COMPACTION-VS-INDUSTRY-2026-06-25.md) — a sourced audit of how fak's cache-prefix-preserving history compaction differs from the built-in compaction in Claude Code, the Anthropic API context-editing feature, Cursor, OpenAI Codex, GitHub Copilot, Aider, and LangChain. Industry mechanisms web-sourced; every fak claim adversarially verified against `internal/agent/anthropic_compact.go` and the gateway. The crux: almost everyone summarizes/clears and re-sends a **rewritten** prompt (which the providers' own docs say breaks the prompt cache); fak drops middle turns and **byte-splices** so the cached prefix stays byte-identical (`bytes.Equal` proven, 13 fail-safe identity exits). Records the four disambiguations that survived adversarial review — the provider cache **cascade** is an unverified hypothesis not a measured fact (the audit's biggest open risk; the `/metrics` instrument exists, it needs one credentialed Anthropic session, [#745](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/745)); fak vs Anthropic context-editing is **orthogonal** (cache-preservation vs recoverable tool-result clearing), not "better"; "lossless" is **overloaded** → a four-axis table (cache-bytes / trust-boundary / model-context / recoverability); Anthropic-wire-shaped **by implementation, not necessity**. Adds the durable surface: a new `client-compaction` dimension in the industry scorecard (a categorical byte-splice **lead** row + an explicit **no-claim** on realized cache reuse).
- [idea-scout triage: attention-located malicious-skill detection (#771) (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-malicious-agent-skills-attention-triage-2026-06-25.md) — triage of arXiv:2606.23416 ("Detecting Malicious Agent Skills in the Wild using Attention", Etteib/Lunghi/Bissyandé): marketplace skills run with user privileges, and the paper's load-bearing claim is that prompt-injection defenses collapse because a skill *is* instructions (the trusted/untrusted-data boundary disappears). Its **Locate-and-Judge** detector scores skill spans by instruction-following **attention** and judges only top-K, auditing a marketplace at order-of-magnitude lower cost. Verdict: **prior art to cite** — it independently validates *why* fak gates the **capability** a skill exercises rather than classifying its instructions (exactly the boundary the paper shows collapses) — plus a real threat whose action step (exfiltrate=send, hijack=destructive call, persist=write) fak's default-deny capability floor already gates a layer **below** a static pre-load scanner. The attention-locator is **not adopted** (best-effort, no kernel load surface, and its attention-is-importance premise is what fak's own [#861](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/861)/[#862](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/862) is still auditing).
- [fak in-kernel CUDA serve on an L4: live tok/s + a concurrency crash fixed (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/L4-INKERNEL-SERVE-AND-CONCURRENCY-FIX-2026-06-25.md) — the reachable-hardware companion to the [GPU server cross-engine](docs/notes/GPU-SERVER-CROSS-ENGINE-DATA-2026-06-25.md) note: with the 8-GPU server nodes reachable only through a down private control bridge, this records what could be measured on the one live GPU node (the `fak-realmodel` GCP L4). Two results. (1) The first **warm steady-state** decode rate against fak's *live* in-kernel CUDA serve: a real Qwen2.5-0.5B Q8_0 decodes at **~466 tok/s** single-stream (median of 8, range 437–510; ~2.25 GB of 23 GB VRAM) — an order of magnitude over the WSL-laptop Q8 numbers, confirming [GPU-QWEN-RESULTS](docs/benchmarks/GPU-QWEN-RESULTS.md)'s thesis that the laptop gap was the WSL launch tax, not the kernel (the *cold* first decode is ~66 tok/s and must not be quoted as steady-state). (2) The serving bug live traffic exposed: a concurrent `/v1/messages` burst **took the GPU serve down** — the single-stream CUDA backend (one `g_stream`, one cuBLAS handle, a shared device free-list) was driven by two concurrent `InKernelPlanner.Complete` goroutines whose per-token op sequences interleaved and corrupted each other's device buffers → sticky `cuda_kernels.cu` illegal-memory-access → poisoned context until restart (the sibling **CPU** serve answered correctly throughout, isolating the fault). Fixed by holding a dedicated `devMu` across the whole forward when a backend is wired (safe queuing, correct for a single-stream device; CPU path untouched), with a **proven non-vacuous** GPU-free test (3719 op-overlaps without the lock, 0 with it). Honestly scoped: a small-model device-decode serving rate, not a 753B / GPU server / stock-engine number.
- [Credit assignment over spans: PRMs, RL credit, agent-memory usefulness (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-credit-assignment-over-spans-2026-06-25.md) — prior-art survey 4/5 for the reward-over-spans epic [#860](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/860): how the RL / agent-training literature assigns credit to intermediate steps and retrieved items, and the span-credit-assignment design those patterns produce. Folds four families into one reward equation — `reward(s,T) = success_gate(T) · Σ γ^(T−t) · m_s(t)` — with each term justified by a named pattern (**outcome gate** from PRMs / Math-Shepherd; **recency discount** from TD(λ) eligibility traces / GAE; **confound normalization** from attention-sink + the "attention is/is-not explanation" contest) and each mapped onto a fak seam that already ships (`ctxplan.OutcomeFromAttention` produces it, `Forecast.Learn` / `Weights.Learn` consume it into the `recall.Page.Utility` Q-slot, `rsiloop` keeps-or-reverts on witnessed signal/noise). Every term is **degenerate-to-current** (identity knobs reproduce the shipped flat cumulative attention) so [#866](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/866) can ablate each independently; carries the epic's honest spine verbatim — attention-as-importance is **contested**, so the producer is a swap point that takes the leave-one-out signal ([#863](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/863)) at the same `Outcome` interface if validation refutes attention, and the outcome gate demands a **witnessed** verdict, never the model's self-report.
- [Shared live trunk vs. branch / worktree / VM-per-agent (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/SHARED-TRUNK-VS-PER-AGENT-ISOLATION-2026-06-25.md) — the positioning half of epic [#822](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/822) / [#834](https://github.com/anthony-chaudhary/fak/issues/834): the field (Cursor background agents, Devin, Sculptor, OpenHands, Dagger `container-use`, Google GitFarm) converged on **never share a tree** — branch/worktree/VM per agent, integration gated on a human merge. That validates fak's contrarian bet (one **live trunk** + **pre-call adjudication** + **disjoint-lease admission**) while giving it **nothing to import** for the hard part, because every surveyed system *avoids* the shared-tree problem rather than solving it. Names the trade as a table (what each side buys/costs), the three named git-admin gaps with their current closure status at HEAD, and carries the epic's honesty caveats **verbatim**: `mvdan/sh` cannot resolve `$VAR`/`alias`/`eval` (fail closed); `grite` = distribution **not** atomicity + the **synthetic** "78%→0%"; a signature is an **identity envelope only** (the SHA already content-addresses); AgenticFlict **sizes the problem, never claims fak's benefit**.
- [Fusion, ref-counting, and GC for the agent loop (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/FUSION-REFCOUNT-GC-FOR-THE-AGENT-LOOP-2026-06-25.md) — the runtime-heap lens on the loop: every turn and tool result is a **heap object**, a pinned goal / active slash-command / open task is a **GC root**, compaction is **garbage collection**. The decisive finding after mapping the tree: the collector machine already ships at the byte/KV depths (`abi.CASPinner` refcount-by-digest, `ProvisionalSink.Promote/Rollback` commit-or-collect, `ctxplan.Forecast.Pins` whose own doc names "the active goal" as the canonical pin, `cachemeta.Lifecycle` TTL+`Touch`-revive) — what is missing is the single **edge from an intentional root to the heap objects it keeps alive**. Locates the gap exactly at `SessionPlanner.pins()` (the complete current root-set is system/first-user/last-user; the goal is absent), defines the **ref-count of a turn** (its live referents: an open goal, a later citing turn, a pending consumer), the conservative over-retain safety asymmetry (a false-free silently loses needed context; a false-retain costs a few tokens), **turn-fusion** (collapse a refcount-1 producer→consumer intermediate, the sound version of `maybeCompactInboundTools`) and **command-tenuring** (a recurring slash-command promotes to a compact rollup — the "store it for aggregate/GC" the goal asked for), and a 4-step first build that wires the goal into the per-turn pin set and measures the effect on the shipped signal/noise metric.
- [vToolcall, forked — best-effort serve-tiers and owning the loop (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/VTOOLCALL-FORK-AND-BESTEFFORT-2026-06-25.md) — the forward-looking sequel to the materialized-view note, answering "best effort" + "fork an open harness (Crush / opencode)". Adds (1) a **consistency-level QoS axis** (`STRICT | BOUNDED_STALE | BEST_EFFORT | SPECULATIVE` threaded as `c.Meta["consistency"]`, a DB-read-consistency dual) with a per-topology graceful-degradation capability matrix, and (2) the **fork direction**: make fak the tool *dispatcher* so the prior note's "impossible on the proxy" verdict flips and serve-as-ran runs closed-loop on real built-in tools. Honest spine, all three stated plainly: forking does not *soften* un-ring, it **relocates** it (after-consumption → a before-consumption write barrier fak owns, needing a net-new suspend-and-resume turn primitive — today's `gateTurn` only terminates); **Crush is FSL-1.1-MIT** (source-available, commercial-restricted at HEAD) so the license-clean call is **build the minimal native TUI on the shipped `internal/agent` seam** (Apache-uniform, single-binary) or host **opencode-as-server** (MIT), never fork Crush into the Apache module; and the loop fak would extend is a **benchmark A/B comparator (`RunArm`) today**, not a production loop. Portfolio verdict: keep the proxy as the universal gate, lead the fork with the native build.
- [vToolcall — tool results as a materialized view (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/VTOOLCALL-MATERIALIZED-VIEW-2026-06-25.md) — the *closed-loop* dual of [vCache](docs/notes/VCACHE-VIRTUAL-API-CACHE-2026-06-24.md): serve a tool result from the kernel's own cache **as if the tool ran**, framed as one materialized view in a query engine over the whole harness-facing surface (tool-defs via `promptmmu`, history via `agent.CompactAnthropicHistory`, context via `contextq`, KV via vCache — and now results via the vDSO). The serve mechanism already ships as the vDSO FastPath (`kernel.go:347` → `abi.FastPaths().Lookup`); this note is its design rationale with **cache invalidation as the spine**. Honest build boundary: result-synthesis is real only where fak *dispatches* the tool (`fak_syscall`/in-kernel), structurally impossible on the `/v1/messages` proxy (the harness runs tools there); witness-keying of the tier-2 key, a cross-process FILL pool, and the speculative-serve `ProvisionalSink` are named **unbuilt**. Law T (sound-before-serve) is the polarity flip from vCache's A2 — a stale tool result *corrupts*, it does not merely *cost*.
- [Fresh status snapshot (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/FRESH-STATUS-2026-06-25.md) — the pinned snapshot of the cross-domain rollup that `tools/fresh_status.py` folds: git context (HEAD/branch/dirty/ahead-behind) + benchmark catalog (run/machine counts, newest-run staleness, per-run **measured | modeled | unknown** provenance with unknown surfaced loudly) + work (plan_audit, zero-state SKIP) + industry parity (`industry_scorecard`), in one `schema/ok/verdict/finding/reason/next_action` control-pane envelope. The sibling of `tools/scorecard_control_pane.py` one level up — that folds the scorecard FAMILY, this folds the four top-level domains. Re-generate with `make status` / `python tools/fresh_status.py --write-doc`.
- [What is an agent? The parts the word smears (2026-06-24)](docs/notes/EXPLAINER-what-is-an-agent-2026-06-24.md) — the *horizontal* complement to [engineering-is-building-loops](docs/explainers/engineering-is-building-loops.md): that doc decomposes an agent by *scale* (the five nested loops), this one decomposes it by *part* (engine, history, context view, system prompt, skill, tool, loop, adjudicator, quarantine, session, identity, communicator), each mapped to a real fak seam cited by symbol + test, not line number. Names the ten cross-cutting *cuts* the word collapses (substrate/view, soft-steering/hard-gate, propose/dispose, replaceable/durable, reasoning/action/answer, primitive/composed, turn/session/identity, drive/audit, single/fleet, residency/reuse/legality) and a glossary of seven overloaded terms (session→5 Go types, agent→4 senses, context→3, model→2, memory→3, tool-vs-skill, steering→3). Honest §6 fences mark the seam-vs-shipped gaps (ctxplan live loop off by default, the evadable detector floor, session `Decide` on the harness arm only, identity as a Python module not an `abi` type, the agent communicator as a pattern not a Go type).

Split into a docs-lane glossary (#721) and a code-lane rename + trust-framing fix (#722, the load-bearing one: stop "agent" naming both the kernel and the untrusted guest). - [GPU server cross-engine data: what runs, how it compares, what's next (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/GPU-SERVER-CROSS-ENGINE-DATA-2026-06-25.md) — a run-log companion to the GLM-5.2 throughput/benchmark plan: the 8-GPU datacenter node runs a stock SGLang server serving `Qwen/Qwen3.6-27B` (tp8, bf16), measured here at **93.05 tok/s single-stream decode / 607.7 tok/s agg @ concurrency 8, 0 errors**. Puts three datacenter-GPU-class numbers on one board with their category boundaries kept honest (real-serving SGLang 27B vs real-serving llama.cpp 753B 2.62 tok/s vs fak *synthetic* kernel cost 13.5–26.5 tok/s — the last is NOT a serving rate and can't sit next to the others), explains why the native fak sweep belongs on the idle 80GB node, and prioritizes next steps (P0 = the same-checkpoint B2 rung now that a live stock server exists + the carried DSA illegal-access bisection; P1 = a chunked-stage fix for the ~4 KB private-control launch limit that silently dropped large remote launches this run). Hardware referred to by class, never lab host/IP/path.
- [vLLM/SGLang pre-agentic assumptions, and the agent-era repairs (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/VLLM-SGLANG-AGENTIC-ASSUMPTIONS-2026-06-25.md) — a source-backed synthesis of the request/cache/scheduler/tool/replica/L3 assumptions serving engines inherited from the chat-completion era, and the fak-shaped repairs: agent-run envelopes, cache hits as verdicts, tool calls as syscalls, authority-aware placement, witnessed completion, and agentic KPIs.

Names the perceived-TTFT split for prose-first, pure tool-call, and all-denied turns.
- [Internal benchmark KPIs, from the agent's point of view (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/AGENTIC-LOOP-KPIS-2026-06-25.md) — an inventory of what fak measures today (work eliminated + cost of one `Decide`), organized by the five-layer loop ladder, then ~30 NEW agentic *lifecycle-latency* KPIs that fill the gap the current catalog is silent on — the numbers an agent author budgets a run against: time to spawn a turn (`ctxplan.RenderTurn`), reap a turn (`ctxmmu.Admit`), query the planner (`ctxplan.Optimize` — today only op-*counts* exist, never wall-clock), checkpoint/fork a session (`sessionimage.DumpDir`), spawn a fan-out wave (`NewBatchFromPrefixReserve`), plus TTFT/TPOT/ITL (now measurable via `agent.StreamingPlanner`) and the rsi keep-rate/cycle-time. Every new KPI is grounded against a real seam with a status tag (standing/modeled/guarded/frozen-sync/proposed) and a one-line honesty fence; flags the four traps (measured≠modeled, latency≠op-count, live≠replay, the frozen-but-sync `Reap`) and a cheapest-to-wire shortlist. Surfaces one cleanup: `preflight_catch_rate` is a declared KPI field `bench.Run` never populates.

- [Frontier agent leaderboard enter/skip map for #1068 — BFCL V4 the pick (2026-06)](docs/notes/FRONTIER-AGENT-LEADERBOARD-SURVEY-2026-06.md) — ranked enter/skip table over BFCL V4 / AA Agentic Index / Galileo v2 / GAIA2 / Vals Index (HAL as cost-axis anchor), each row carrying a dated source URL, the cost to run, the exact fak-attributable number that would ride it, and a WITNESSED-vs-OBSERVED label. Pick = **BFCL V4**, the only board with both a USD-cost and a latency-seconds column, so a held-accuracy-constant fak-in-vs-fak-out cost delta (~3% gateway tax; KV-reuse marginal-over-TUNED 4.1× on a 50×5 fleet, both WITNESSED) is postable against a real public column — fak rides the cost column, never the accuracy rank. Records the negative result that **no public board in this family scores a tool-call adjudication gateway's safe-completion / policy-compliance / provable-deletion as a first-class axis**, so fak's model-free ASR-0.000 floor has no leaderboard home. Names the next-step run-contract stub `experiments/agent-live/bfcl-v4-raw-fak-contract-20260627.json` (`result_claim_allowed=false`).
- [Long-running agent loops: make fak the loop kernel, not another cron (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/LONG-RUNNING-AGENT-LOOPS-2026-06-25.md) — the operating-system-shaped strategy for rolling cron/launchd/Scheduled Tasks, cloud and remote nodes, phone/Slack/WhatsApp control, notifications, foreground vs background sessions, and green-thread-like logical loops into one fak surface. The key split: schedulers and chat are interrupt sources; fak should own loop identity, admission, leases, run ledgers, task snapshots, witnessed completion, notification discipline, and cache-aware scheduling. First shipped slice: `internal/loopmgr` plus `fak loop append|run|status` provide the hash-chained loop-event ledger, OS-scheduler wrapper, and fold; `tools/issue_resolve_dispatch.py` and `tools/issue_resolve_progress.py` are the first producers; `tools/register_issue_dispatch.ps1` is the first Task Scheduler adapter migrated through `fak loop run`, while Mac dogfood and other scheduler paths remain next.
- [The gardening bundle: default-on the cleanup we run by hand (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/GARDENING-BUNDLE-DEFAULT-ON-2026-06-25.md) — the gardening-workload companion to the loop-kernel note. Its original Python fold was retired after the Go port; the current entry point is `fak garden`, backed by `internal/gardenbundle`, with `make garden` and the scheduled garden workflow preserving the bounded read-only fold and brake.
- [Inbound prompt-MMU: curate WHAT enters the window (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/INBOUND-PROMPT-MMU-2026-06-25.md) — the INGRESS dual of the result-side `ctxmmu`. fak gates the outbound/result half of the context window end-to-end (quarantine results, adjudicate calls, compact old turns) but the inbound composition — tool DEFINITIONS, system, injected skills/memory — forwards byte-identical on the `fak guard -- claude` passthrough. The epic + rungs to curate the ingress, on-by-default both directions with flags only for opinionated overrides, all under the #555 cache-prefix byte-splice + fail-safe-identity discipline. Rung 1 (`internal/promptmmu` `CompactInboundTools`, denied-tool-def pruning) SHIPPED; cache geometry forces it to fire at the session-start/RESET prefix rebuild, refusing pre-breakpoint prunes that would bust the session cache.
- [The system-prompt MMU: fak owns its own base context (2026-06-29)](docs/notes/SYSTEM-PROMPT-MMU-2026-06-29.md) — the AUTHORSHIP dual of the inbound prompt-MMU. #751 prunes a harness-authored prompt as it passes through; this epic takes ownership of the *composition* of the head — a fak-first base context where the irreducible resident spine is fak's own concepts (the attention-sink anchor, byte-identical every turn) and the harness's rules/skill-menu/environment become a queried, paged, append-after-the-breakpoint overlay assembled from the same capability substrate the skill loader (#1103) already ships. The two end-states: (a) the minimal base where the resident head is *only* fak plus a retrieval affordance, and (b) the live base where the prompt is a first-class object fak rewrites at runtime by page-in+mask under a witness gate. The cache doctrine is REGIME-SCOPED, not universal: "append-and-mask, never edit-in-place" is a hard law only where fak does *not* own the cache (the provider passthrough and the proxy-to-local-engine regimes, whose radix-tree-keyed prefix cache goes cold the moment the head changes — Manus' measured ~10×); when fak owns the engine it keeps the pre-RoPE `Kraw` plane and can mutate the head while keeping survivors reusable via **bit-exact span deletion + re-RoPE** (witnessed `max|Δ|=0`, live behind `FAK_INKERNEL_KVMMU`) — though relocated-span *reuse* is not shipped (the repo's own audit faults it to recompute). Grounds the fak-first inversion in attention geometry (regime-independent) plus a cache-stability reason on the passthrough path, states the five load-bearing invariants (spine byte-identical on the provider path; append-and-mask in the provider-uncontrolled regime; safety-critical always resident; pointers-not-bodies with a bounded index; self-modification witness-gated, versioned, append-mostly — the agent never rewrites its spine), maps the built-but-request-path-dead substrate this would *wire* rather than reinvent (`cachemeta.PromptSegment`/`promptmmu`/`ctxplan`/`capindex`/`contextq`/`ctxmmu`/`ctxresidency`/`sessionreset` plus the regime-B `model`/`radixkv`/`kvmmu` span machinery), and lays out six rungs from a new `internal/syspromptmmu` spine to the `fak debug` re-derivation surface that feeds #1217. Planning + tickets only.
- [Bottleneck map and durable process loops (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/BOTTLENECK-MAP-2026-06-25.md) — the current system + GitHub/open-work bottleneck map, with the exact read-only/ignored-artifact commands used to refresh it and the public/private publication boundary for future repeats.

The crux: Apple and Google both ship an on-device model with a typed tool/intent contract but leave adversarial enforcement in-process (Android even auto-authorizes "related sub-tools" from one grant), and fak's SHA-256 hash-chained journal already structurally matches the EU AI Act Article 12 audit-log mandate landing Aug 2026. Every claim cites a shipped artifact (cosine=1.0 parity, 3-tier offline, the swappable EngineDriver seam, Qwen2.5-1.5B adjudicated on CPU) or a dated source; names the net-new work honestly — no published linux/arm64 binary (one matrix line), no Pi/Jetson footprint witness, no NDK/Swift bindings, no NPU adapter.
- [Serving the strongest model on a MacBook + cross-laptop agentic bench (2026-06-24)](docs/notes/MACBOOK-SERVE-AND-AGENTIC-BENCH-2026-06-24.md) — what the pure fak stack can serve on Apple Silicon today and how to A/B it agentically from a second laptop, grounded in shipped code + on-device M3 Pro numbers. The crux: fak's own forward is purest but slowest (Qwen2.5-7B Q8 @ ~8.7 tok/s is the agentically-usable ceiling), the gateway-in-front-of-Ollama path is fast but the inference isn't fak's. Corrects a first-draft error — `fak swebench run --agent fleet --gateway <mac-ip>:8080` already ships the dataset-scored cross-laptop runner (real SWE-bench Verified RESOLVED N/Total); the only genuine residual is a Docker-free Mac-hostable grade so the loop closes on two laptops alone.
- [vCache — a virtual API cache over providers we don't control (2026-06-24)](docs/notes/VCACHE-VIRTUAL-API-CACHE-2026-06-24.md) — the active control plane proposed *on top of* the passive `internal/cachemeta` tier: treat provider prompt-caching as virtual memory we can observe but not address (vBlock = virtual page, a warm provider prefix = physical frame, the manifest = page table, the request scheduler = MMU), and recall a unit by shaping the *order, shape, and timing* of requests so the provider serves the prefix from its own cache. Audit-grounded: every `cachemeta` symbol checked against HEAD (the fabricated `CacheLane` reference removed; the real seam is `ShapeGLMTurnSegmentWitnessed` + `glm_coherence.go`), the Anthropic per-model min-prefix corrected to 1024 (Opus 4.8 / Sonnet 4.6 / 4.5; 4096 is Haiku 4.5), and the economics (break-even N≥2/≥3, Zipf `s≈1.74` for "7 anchors→85%", chain-horizon `C₀=103`, the 300× recall-by-rebuild loss) independently re-derived. Phased M1–M5 plan + four adversarial laws (warmth is a belief never a boolean; fingerprint the wire bytes; the warm primitive has an irreducible race; calibrate-don't-assume on implicit providers).
- [Budget-triggered session reset (2026-06-25)](docs/notes/BUDGET-TRIGGERED-SESSION-RESET-2026-06-25.md) — the long-context reset design and shipped slice: context budget exhaustion mints a continuation id, `--reset-on-budget` distills a carryover seed and re-arms the fresh trace, while live KV splice and hard child-process relaunch remain named follow-ons.
- [Agent optimization methods — field inventory, survey & index (2026-06-23)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-agent-optimization-methods-survey-2026-06-23.md) — 370 distinct methods across 16 families spanning the whole stack (reasoning, test-time compute, context/memory, retrieval, KV reuse, tools, planning, multi-agent, routing, serving, training, reliability), each with a one-line definition + what it optimizes + maturity, plus an alphabetical master index. The umbrella that cross-links the deep notes ([agentic caching](docs/notes/AGENTIC-CACHING-SOTA-2026-06-19.md), [scaling laws](docs/notes/SCALING-LAWS-OF-AGENTS-2026-06-19.md), [O(1) context economics](docs/explainers/o1-context-window-economics.md)).
- [Three layers of agent optimization — skill, trajectory, substrate (2026-06-24)](docs/notes/RESEARCH-three-layers-of-agent-optimization-2026-06-24.md) — the conceptual companion to the field inventory above: separates *what* an agent should do (skill), *how any run unfolds* abstract over skill (trajectory), and *what is kept hot* (substrate), then spends its length on the three differences. Uses only the refined claims that survived an adversarial pass — the "harness carries the weak agent" convergence is true on a live re-decode and false for a frozen trajectory (fak's astropy 1/1→0 counterexample + the `turnbench/ope.go` divergence frontier); trajectory↔substrate is a complement not an adjoint; the compiler-stack analogy is a leaky gradient because fak fuses the safety and reuse boundary. Closes on the LIVE-vs-DORMANT `req.Raw` passthrough gap.
- [Agentic caching SOTA (2026-06-19)](docs/notes/AGENTIC-CACHING-SOTA-2026-06-19.md)
- [Planned view measured over real transcripts (2026-06-23)](docs/notes/CTXPLAN-REAL-TRANSCRIPT-MEASUREMENT-2026-06-23.md) — the empirical counterpart to scaling.go's synthetic model: replays the heaviest real Claude Code sessions turn-by-turn through the real planner + page-fault handler. 13.3× fewer resident tokens than linear; 31.7% forecast-miss rate with 100% of misses served; exact recall 715/715 turns vs compaction losing facts on 695/715. Closes #559 (`cmd/ctxplanbench`).
- [ctxview default-on witness (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/CTXVIEW-DEFAULT-ON-WITNESS-2026-06-28.md) — the GPU-free, re-runnable evidence that the O(1) planner (`--ctx-view-budget`) is correct and fail-open enough to flip from off-by-default to a conservative default-on: `ctxplanbench -selfcheck` (resident 16/20, exact recall 5/5, faults 3/3 served, bounded-probe == full-scan), the on-the-wire `TestCtxViewHTTP` (OFF byte-equals inbound; ON keeps the Anthropic cache prefix byte-identical with exact recall), and the fleet's realized-reuse dogfood (75.1% from #1114). Names the residual: the flip is blocked on `cmd/fak` building (peer WIP).
- [Dynamic ctxview budget — 8000 is below the agentic floor (2026-06-28)](docs/notes/CTXVIEW-DYNAMIC-BUDGET-2026-06-28.md) — why the flat `--ctx-view-budget 8000` default is the wrong absolute number for agentic sessions (below the faithfulness floor and far below the ~34K economic crossover), the [pins+recency .. ~12%-of-billed-prompt] band the budget lives in, and a workload-adaptive policy that scales the O(1) resident target to the model's context window (prefix-floored, economics-aware control-loop follow-on). The next-steps ladder for the ctxview epic.
- [Planner per-turn compute flatten, measured (2026-06-23)](docs/notes/CTXPLAN-PLANNING-COST-FLATTEN-2026-06-23.md) — the sibling that measures the planner's OWN work, not just its output: a persistent `ctxplan.Index` bounds the per-turn probe (Θ(N²)→Θ(c·N)) over the same real sessions. 851 turns: peak-probe ≤ cap on every turn, 1.5× less planner work (growing with N), resident-token efficiency preserved (within 1.2%), and the honest cost↔fidelity finding — the bounded probe is byte-identical to the full scan when it sees the whole candidate set (342/342) but diverges at default width on long sessions (every divergence a served page-fault, never a lost fact). #558/#559 (`cmd/ctxplanbench`).
- [Context-window baseline + self-reduce 2× (2026-06-23)](docs/notes/CTXWIN-CONTEXT-WINDOW-BASELINE-2026-06-23.md) — what a Claude Code session's context window is actually made of, and how much halves at low risk vs bounded-loss. The empirical pass over real transcripts (`tools/ctxwin.py`) the [O(1)-turn planner](docs/notes/O1-TURN-CONTEXT-PLANNER-2026-06-23.md) names as its missing measurement.
- [Multiple-sink transmission for the cache lifecycle (2026-06-23)](docs/notes/MULTI-SINK-CACHE-LIFECYCLE-2026-06-23.md) — is one kernel op fanning Promote/Rollback across every registered `ProvisionalSink` useful for the KV/context cache lifecycle? Yes, but latent: one registrant today, the fan never exercised with >1 sink (historically witnessed in the since-retired `internal/spec` composition leaf), and best-effort not atomic. Ranks which cache tiers should become sinks next.

Sound, fail-safe-to-RETAIN, fills a real gap (the keep-but-park dual of `retire`) and matches spec (PARKED→exit 3, LIVE→0, INDETERMINATE→4) — but **latent**: nothing routes on the verdict, the CLI is single-item only (the no-loss `partition` fold is import-only), the evidence bits are host-produced.
